Token-billed\n// providers (OpenAI gpt-4o-*, Gemini) bill from the response `usage` object so\n// the charge is exact; duration-billed providers bill from returned audio\n// seconds. See shared-app-classification/cloud-stt-catalog.json for the\n// client-facing credits/min surface.\n\n// OpenAI — whisper-1 is duration-billed; gpt-4o-* are token-billed.\nconst OPENAI_WHISPER1_COST_PER_AUDIO_MINUTE = 0.006;\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_TRANSCRIBE_INPUT_COST_PER_TOKEN = 2.50 / 1_000_000;\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_TRANSCRIBE_OUTPUT_COST_PER_TOKEN = 10.00 / 1_000_000;\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_MINI_TRANSCRIBE_INPUT_COST_PER_TOKEN = 1.25 / 1_000_000;\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_MINI_TRANSCRIBE_OUTPUT_COST_PER_TOKEN = 5.00 / 1_000_000;\n// Per-minute floors used only when the token `usage` object is missing/changed,\n// so a gpt-4o transcription never bills $0 (fail-closed).\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_TRANSCRIBE_FLOOR_PER_MINUTE = 0.006;\nconst OPENAI_GPT4O_MINI_TRANSCRIBE_FLOOR_PER_MINUTE = 0.003;\n\n// Gemini — audio billed at 32 tokens/sec; cost read from usageMetadata. Input\n// is multi-modal: TEXT (the instruction+vocab prompt) and AUDIO bill at\n// DIFFERENT per-token rates on Flash models, so both must be tracked. Pro models\n// (2.5-pro, 3.1-pro) bill every input modality at one rate but switch to a\n// higher long-context tier above 200k prompt tokens. Rates: Standard paid tier,\n// per-1M ÷ 1e6. Ref: https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/pricing\ninterface GeminiRate {\n  textInputPerToken: number;\n  audioInputPerToken: number;\n  outputPerToken: number;\n  // Override rates for prompts above GEMINI_LONG_CONTEXT_THRESHOLD tokens.\n  // Present only for models with tiered (Pro) pricing; flat models omit it.\n  longContext?: { textInputPerToken: number; audioInputPerToken: number; outputPerToken: number };\n}\nconst GEMINI_LONG_CONTEXT_THRESHOLD = 200_000;\nconst M = 1_000_000;\nconst GEMINI_RATES: Record<string, GeminiRate> = {\n  'gemini-2.5-flash': { textInputPerToken: 0.30 / M, audioInputPerToken: 1.00 / M, outputPerToken: 2.50 / M },\n  'gemini-2.5-flash-lite': { textInputPerToken: 0.10 / M, audioInputPerToken: 0.30 / M, outputPerToken: 0.40 / M },\n  'gemini-2.5-pro': {\n    textInputPerToken: 1.25 / M, audioInputPerToken: 1.25 / M, outputPerToken: 10.00 / M,\n    longContext: { textInputPerToken: 2.50 / M, audioInputPerToken: 2.50 / M, outputPerToken: 15.00 / M },\n  },\n  'gemini-3-flash-preview': { textInputPerToken: 0.50 / M, audioInputPerToken: 1.00 / M, outputPerToken: 3.00 / M },\n  'gemini-3.1-pro-preview': {\n    textInputPerToken: 2.00 / M, audioInputPerToken: 2.00 / M, outputPerToken: 12.00 / M,\n    longContext: { textInputPerToken: 4.00 / M, audioInputPerToken: 4.00 / M, outputPerToken: 18.00 / M },\n  },\n};\nconst GEMINI_FALLBACK_RATE = GEMINI_RATES['gemini-2.5-flash'];\n=== utils roundToTenth ===\n25:export function roundToTenth(value: number): number {\n26-  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) {\n27-    return 0;\n28-  }\n29-\n30-  const rounded = Math.round((value + Number.EPSILON) * 10) / 10;\n31-  return Math.abs(rounded) < Number.EPSILON ? {"parentUuid":"a006058c-8f75-4406-8679-81665fabccc5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"77ec06fb-aafc-4d6b-b92c-3296ca279672","agentId":"aa6e51c3020cff7b0","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01X1by9nS1AMqWQ1kgDMRCeb","type":"tool_result","content":"7:export const LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60; // 1 hour for valid and invalid keys\n\nexport async function validateAuth(input: AuthInput, forceRefresh = false): Promise<AuthResult> {\n  const { licenseKey } = input;\n\n  // HyperWhisper Cloud is licensed-only: a valid license key (which carries the\n  // credit balance) is required for every request. There is no anonymous/trial\n  // path — without a key the request is rejected before any provider work.\n  if (!licenseKey) {\n    console.log('[Auth] No license_key provided');\n    return { ok: false, response: licenseRequiredResponse() };\n  }\n\n  const maskedKey = maskLicenseKey(licenseKey);\n\n  if (!forceRefresh) {\n    const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n    if (cached) {\n      console.log(`[Auth] Cache HIT for ${maskedKey}: valid=${cached.isValid}, credits=${cached.credits}, cachedAt=${cached.cachedAt}`);\n      if (!cached.isValid) {\n        return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() };\n      }\n      return {\n        ok: true,\n        value: {\n          identifier: licenseKey,\n          credits: cached.credits,\n          licenseKey,\n        },\n      };\n    }\n    console.log(`[Auth] Cache MISS for ${maskedKey}, calling API...`);\n  } else {\n    console.log(`[Auth] Force refresh for ${maskedKey}, bypassing cache...`);\n  }\n\n  const validation = await validateLicenseViaApi(licenseKey);\n  if (!validation.isValid) {\n    console.log(`[Auth] License ${maskedKey} is INVALID`);\n    return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() };\n  }\n\n  console.log(`[Auth] License ${maskedKey} is VALID with ${validation.credits} credits`);\n  return {\n    ok: true,\n    value: {\n      identifier: licenseKey,\n      credits: validation.credits,\n      licenseKey,\n    },\n  };\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"8ec641d2-06aa-481c-a816-73e3895b0162","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:42:17.954Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"7:export const LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60; // 1 hour for valid and invalid keys\n\nexport async function validateAuth(input: AuthInput, forceRefresh = false): Promise<AuthResult> {\n  const { licenseKey } = input;\n\n  // HyperWhisper Cloud is licensed-only: a valid license key (which carries the\n  // credit balance) is required for every request. There is no anonymous/trial\n  // path — without a key the request is rejected before any provider work.\n  if (!licenseKey) {\n    console.log('[Auth] No license_key provided');\n    return { ok: false, response: licenseRequiredResponse() };\n  }\n\n  const maskedKey = maskLicenseKey(licenseKey);\n\n  if (!forceRefresh) {\n    const cached = await getCachedLicense(licenseKey);\n    if (cached) {\n      console.log(`[Auth] Cache HIT for ${maskedKey}: valid=${cached.isValid}, credits=${cached.credits}, cachedAt=${cached.cachedAt}`);\n      if (!cached.isValid) {\n        return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() };\n      }\n      return {\n        ok: true,\n        value: {\n          identifier: licenseKey,\n          credits: cached.credits,\n          licenseKey,\n        },\n      };\n    }\n    console.log(`[Auth] Cache MISS for ${maskedKey}, calling API...`);\n  } else {\n    console.log(`[Auth] Force refresh for ${maskedKey}, bypassing cache...`);\n  }\n\n  const validation = await validateLicenseViaApi(licenseKey);\n  if (!validation.isValid) {\n    console.log(`[Auth] License ${maskedKey} is INVALID`);\n    return { ok: false, response: invalidLicenseResponse() };\n  }\n\n  console.log(`[Auth] License ${maskedKey} is VALID with ${validation.credits} credits`);\n  return {\n    ok: true,\n    value: {\n      identifier: licenseKey,\n      credits: validation.credits,\n      licenseKey,\n    },\n  };\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a006058c-8f75-4406-8679-81665fabccc5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"36ec1e6f-3663-4f75-a1f6-e06d37c2ce6d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"aa6e51c3020cff7b0","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWHnScHBe8FwTa9m8vLB","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I have the full picture. Here are the seam bugs found along T6.\n\n---\n\n## T6 — credit ledger endpoint (`/api/license/credits`) findings\n\n---\n\n### B1 — Partial spend is reported as a full deduction (silent overdraft forgiveness)\n\n**trace**\n1. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/transcribe.ts:679` `deductCredits(auth, result.costUsd, …)` (fire-and-forget)\n2. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/middleware/credits.ts:144` `recordLicenseUsage(auth.identifier, creditsUsed, metadata)`\n3. `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/route.ts:119` `newCredits = await deductCreditBalance(license.userId, amount)`\n4. `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:626→627` `deductCreditBalance` → `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance(userId, amount)`\n5. `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:583-607` loop: `remainingToDeduct` may still be `> 0` when the grant rows run out — the loop just ends. It returns `{ balance, deductedAmount }` where `deductedAmount < amount`.\n6. `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:628` `return result.balance` — **`deductedAmount` is dropped on the floor.**\n7. `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/route.ts:133-136` returns HTTP **200** with `credits_deducted: amount` — the *requested* amount, not the amount actually taken.\n\n**seam** — `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` (db-layer.ts:555) has **partial-spend, floor-at-zero** semantics and faithfully reports `deductedAmount`. Its only caller `deductCreditBalance` (db-layer.ts:626) and the route (route.ts:119) have **all-or-nothing** semantics and echo back the input. Neither layer has any \"insufficient balance\" error contract at all. The route's own comment at :117 (\"Atomic SQL decrement (floored at 0)\") documents the floor and then ignores it.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n```\nPOST /api/license/credits\n{\"license_key\":\"HW-...\",\"amount\":2000,\"metadata\":{}}\n```\nagainst a wallet whose active grants sum to `0.10`. Response:\n```\n200 {\"credits_remaining\": 0, \"credits_deducted\": 2000}\n```\nReachable from the product without any hostile intent: `estimateCreditsFromSize` (credits.ts:22-39) assumes 64 kbps (`BYTES_PER_MINUTE_ESTIMATE = 480_000`, constants.ts:11). A 1-hour 6 kbps Opus upload is ~2.7 MB → estimated ~5.6 audio-minutes → gate passes at ~35 credits, while the real 60-minute Gemini-Pro transcription bills thousands of credits (`USD_PER_CREDIT = 0.001`, cost-calculator.ts:168).\n\n**consequence** — The operator eats the entire overage in real provider dollars, and every observability surface says it was billed: the API returns 200 with the full amount, `route.ts:128` logs `Credits deducted: 2000`, and the cloud logs no warning. There is no metric anywhere that would ever surface the loss. Per-request loss is bounded only by `MAX_CREDIT_DEDUCTION_AMOUNT = 1e6` credits = $1,000.\n\n**confidence** high\n\n---\n\n### B2 — 1-hour cached balance + post-work fire-and-forget deduction = unbounded concurrent overdraft\n\n**trace**\n1. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/constants.ts:7` `LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60 * 60` (1 hour)\n2. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/middleware/auth.ts:110-121` cache HIT returns `credits: cached.credits` with no freshness check\n3. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/middleware/credits.ts:46-49` `validateCredits` compares `roundToTenth(auth.credits)` against the estimate — this is the **only** balance gate in the system\n4. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/transcribe.ts:418` gate passes → provider work runs\n5. `transcribe.ts:679` `deductCredits(...)` fired **without `await`** (`credits.ts:118-130` returns the promise; callers do `.catch(console.error)`)\n6. `credits.ts:85-91` the Redis cache is only refreshed *after* the deduction round-trip completes — i.e. after the response has already been served\n\n**seam** — `route.ts` POST is genuinely atomic per-request (FOR UPDATE at db-layer.ts:580) and its comment claims concurrency safety. But the actual admission-control decision lives an entire network hop away in `credits.ts:41` `validateCredits`, against a number that can be up to 3600 s stale and is never decremented optimistically. There is **no reservation / hold** anywhere — credits are only ever debited after the money has already been spent with the upstream provider.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — one license key with 5 credits; fire 100 concurrent `POST /transcribe` within the same second (or any time inside the 1 h cache window before the first deduction lands).\n\n**consequence** — All 100 pass the gate reading `credits: 5`. Total debited across all 100: 5 credits (B1 floors the rest at zero). The operator pays 100 transcriptions' worth of ElevenLabs/Gemini/OpenAI spend for 5 credits of revenue. Nothing in the system distinguishes this from normal traffic.\n\n**confidence** high\n\n---\n\n### B3 — A failed deduction both loses the charge *and* preserves the stale cache, opening a 1-hour free-usage window\n\n**trace**\n1. `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/middleware/credits.ts:74-82` — any non-2xx (`400` unknown key / `400` \"License is revoked\" / `400` bad amount / `409` deduction failed / `500`) → `console.warn` → `return`. All four error contracts from `route.ts` (`:87`, `:102`, `:109`, `:123`, `:141`) collapse into one branch.\n2. `credits.ts:92-96` — network error / `AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)` → `console.warn` → swallowed.\n3. `credits.ts:85-91` — `cacheLicense` is inside the success branch only. **On any failure the stale Redis entry survives untouched for the remainder of its 1 h TTL.**\n4. `performDeduction` (credits.ts:132-146) has no retry, no dead-letter, no persistence. `drainPendingDeductions` (:106-116) only waits on the in-flight promise — it re-drives nothing.\n\n**seam** — `route.ts` POST distinguishes four failure modes with distinct status codes and messages; `recordLicenseUsage` (credits.ts:53) treats all of them, plus network failure, plus timeout, as \"log and carry on\". A permanent failure is indistinguishable from a transient one, so the system neither retries nor stops serving.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — Revoke a license key mid-session (`account_keys.status != 'granted'`). Auth still cache-HITs for up to an hour (`auth.ts:110`); every deduction now gets `400 {\"error\":\"License is revoked\"}` from `route.ts:107-112`; the cloud warns and keeps transcribing. Equivalently: a 10 s Next.js cold start on the deduction POST, or a Fly `SIGKILL` past the drain timeout.\n\n**consequence** — Up to an hour of completely free, unmetered transcription at operator cost, per key, with the only evidence being a `console.warn` line. Even in the benign single-request case, the charge is simply gone — Fly machine recycles and Vercel cold starts are routine, so this is steady-state revenue leakage, not an edge case.\n\n**confidence** high\n\n---\n\n### B4 — The money sink has no authentication, no rate limit, and no idempotency key; the read side leaks `stripe_customer_id`\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/middleware.ts:159` `matcher: [\"/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\\\..*).*)\"]` — `/api/*` is **explicitly excluded** from all middleware. No auth layer, no rate limiter, nothing runs before the route.\n2. `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/route.ts:80-96` — the only credential is `license_key` in the JSON body; no shared secret, no HMAC, no `NEXTJS_INTERNAL_TOKEN`, no IP allowlist for the cloud caller.\n3. `route.ts:83, :130` — `metadata` is destructured, never validated, and passed only to `console.log`. Confirmed no usage/ledger table exists (`metadata` appears in `db-layer.ts` only at `:934`, inside `getOrCreateUser`). **There is no idempotency key anywhere in the deduction path.**\n4. `route.ts:31-64` GET — unauthenticated, takes `license_key` as a **URL query parameter**, returns `{credits, stripe_customer_id}`.\n5. `nextjs/app/api/account/credits/route.ts:12` — `export { GET, POST }`, so both are live on a second path too.\n\n**seam** — the route's own header comment (`route.ts:21-24`) asserts \"The license key itself acts as authentication\". That is a *confidentiality* claim the surrounding system does not honour: the GET puts the key in a query string, so it is recorded in Vercel/CDN access logs, browser history, and `Referer` headers, and `auth.ts:60` / `route.ts:128` log truncated keys while the cloud sends the full key in a request body over the public internet.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n```bash\n# wipe, no auth, no rate limit, confirmed by the response body:\ncurl -X POST https://…/api/account/credits \\\n  -H 'content-type: application/json' \\\n  -d '{\"license_key\":\"HW-XXXX-…\",\"amount\":1000000}'\n# -> {\"credits_remaining\":0,\"credits_deducted\":1000000}\n\n# unauthenticated wallet + Stripe-customer recon:\ncurl 'https://…/api/license/credits?license_key=HW-XXXX-…'\n# -> {\"credits\":0,\"stripe_customer_id\":\"cus_…\"}\n```\n\n**consequence** — One request permanently zeroes a paying customer's wallet, and the response body confirms success. The victim has no recourse and no audit trail: `metadata` is unvalidated and unpersisted, so there is no record of *who* spent the credits or *what for* — there is no way to reconstruct or reverse the theft. Separately, a leaked key yields the customer's Stripe customer id to anyone. And because there is no idempotency key, a genuine duplicate delivery (client resend, proxy retry, drain re-run) double-charges a real user with no dedup.\n\n**confidence** high\n\n---\n\n### B5 — Refunding an **expired** grant claws the credits out of a different, valid, unrefunded pack\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts:553` (or `:600`) → `refundCreditGrant({sourceType, sourceId})`\n2. `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:455-466` locate the refunded grant `FOR UPDATE` — this SELECT deliberately has **no expiry filter**, so an expired grant is a valid refund target\n3. `db-layer.ts:476-481` `clawback = originalAmount - alreadyRefunded` (full purchased value)\n4. `db-layer.ts:489-492` `toClawback = Math.min(clawback, await getActiveGrantsTotal(tx, grant.user_id))` — `getActiveGrantsTotal` (:313-320) applies `ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY` (:300), so **the refunded grant's own remaining value is excluded from this clamp**; the clamp is sized against *other* grants\n5. `db-layer.ts:501-514` the drawdown query also applies `AND ${ACTIVE_GRANT_EXPIRY}` (:507), so the `CASE WHEN id = ${grant.id} THEN 0 ELSE 1 END` \"refunded grant first\" preference (:509) **is a no-op** — the expired grant isn't in the result set at all. The whole clawback lands on the next rows: `expires_at ASC, created_at ASC`.\n\n**seam** — `refundCreditGrant`'s target lookup (`:455`, expiry-agnostic) versus its clamp and drawdown (`:491`, `:507`, expiry-enforcing). The comment at `:499-500` states the intended invariant exactly — *\"Expired grants are skipped: they no longer back any spendable balance, so clawing them back would remove value that was never available\"* — but the clamp at `:489` measures the ceiling against the wrong set, so the code does the opposite of what the comment promises.\n\n**trigger_sketch**\n- Jan 2025: user buys pack A (5000 credits, `expires_at` = Jan 2026 via `CREDIT_GRANT_TTL_MS`, db-layer.ts:373/379).\n- Feb 2026: A has lapsed with 5000 `remaining_amount` still on the row. User buys pack B (5000 credits, active, unspent).\n- Mar 2026: a `charge.refunded` webhook fires for pack A.\n- `clawback = 5000`; `getActiveGrantsTotal` = 5000 (B only); `toClawback = 5000`; drawdown returns **only B** → B drained to 0, `status = 'spent'`.\n\n**consequence** — The user is refunded cash for A (which was already worthless) **and** loses the 5000 credits of pack B that they paid for separately and were never refunded for. A dollars-out, credits-out double loss to the customer. `refundCreditGrant` returns `refundedAmount: clawback` (`:550`, the *unclamped* value), so the webhook and admin UI both report a clean full refund. Same shape fires when A is fully spent rather than expired — but that case is documented as intentional at `:449-454`; the expired case is not.\n\n**confidence** medium-high\n\n---\n\n### B6 — \"Null means never expires\" is unreachable; every grant is force-stamped, using app-server clock\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/src/db/schema/credit-grants.ts` — `expiresAt` comment: *\"Null means never expires (e.g. trial credits)\"*\n2. `nextjs/src/lib/db-layer.ts:379` `const expiresAt = new Date(Date.now() + CREDIT_GRANT_TTL_MS);` — unconditional\n3. `db-layer.ts:390` `expiresAt` written on **every** insert path, and `CreditGrantInsert` carries no expiry field to override it\n4. Affected callers: `db-layer.ts:275` `sourceType: \"internal_bundle\"` (the free bundle minted with every new key), `customers.ts:228` `admin_license_bundle` (5000 credits), `customers.ts:251` `admin_manual`\n\n**seam** — the schema documents a nullable-expiry contract for trial/comped credits; `grantCreditLotInTransaction` (the sole insert path) never honours it. Also note the expiry instant is computed with `Date.now()` on the Next.js app server, while enforcement is `expires_at > now()` evaluated on the Postgres clock (`db-layer.ts:300`) — a string ↔ instant boundary across two clocks.\n\n**consequence** — Comped, trial, and admin-goodwill credits silently vanish 365 days after issue with no notification, no state change (`status` stays `'active'`), and no UI signal other than the derived `expired` flag. A support agent looking at the row sees `status: active` and a non-zero `remaining_amount`, while `getCreditBalance` reports 0 — the classic \"the customer says they have credits, the dashboard says active, the API says zero\" ticket with no way to explain it.\n\n**confidence** medium\n\n---\n\n### B7 — The 2-decimal-place guard exists on exactly one of three writers\n\n**trace**\n1. `nextjs/app/api/license/credits/validation.ts:18-20` rejects sub-scale amounts, with a comment (`:3-6`) explaining precisely why: *\"numeric(_, 2) … a sub-cent deduction (e.g. 0.005) can net a zero deduction\"*\n2. `nextjs/server/api/routers/admin/customers.ts:251-253` `addCredits` input is `z.number().positive().max(MAX_ADMIN_CREDIT_GRANT)` — **no scale check**\n3. → `grantCreditLot` → `db-layer.ts:386-387` `originalAmount: data.amount.toString()` into `numeric(20, 2)` → Postgres rounds half-up\n4. → `db-layer.ts:360` `balance: sql\\`${creditBalances.balance} + ${amount}\\`` → also rounded by the column\n5. Same gap on the Stripe path: `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` (`db-layer.ts:414`) passes `data.creditAmount` straight through with no scale validation\n\n**seam** — the deduction path tightened its contract to the column's scale; the two *credit-issuing* paths never learned about it. The invariant \"every amount reaching `numeric(_,2)` round-trips at 2dp\" holds on the sink and not on either source.\n\n**trigger_sketch** — admin `addCredits({licenseKeyId, amount: 10.005})`.\n\n**consequence** — 10.01 credits are created from a 10.005 grant (Postgres rounds half-up), while the mutation's return value reports `addedAmount: 10.005` next to `newBalance` read back from the rounded column — the two disagree in the same response object. Small per-event, but it is credit creation from rounding, on the path with the loosest validation, and it is exactly the failure class the sink-side validator was written to prevent.\n\n**confidence** medium\n\n---\n\n### B8 — Grant arithmetic crosses the JS-float boundary on every hop; `=== 0` decides the terminal state\n\n**trace**\n1. `db-layer.ts:590` `const grantRemaining = Number(grant.remaining_amount)` — `numeric` → JS float\n2. `db-layer.ts:595` `const newRemaining = grantRemaining - deduction` — IEEE754 subtraction\n3. `db-layer.ts:599` `remainingAmount: newRemaining.toString()` — a up-to-17-significant-digit float literal is handed back to `numeric(20,2)`, which re-rounds it half-up\n4. `db-layer.ts:600` `status: newRemaining === 0 ? \"spent\" : \"active\"` — exact float equality decides the terminal state\n5. Identical pattern in `refundCreditGrant` at `:518-526`\n\n**seam** — the column's scale-2 rounding is the *only* thing keeping the ledger clean; the application layer does its arithmetic at full float width and writes the residue back. `0.30 - 0.10` produces `0.19999999999999998`, written as that literal and rounded to `0.20`; a residue landing on exactly `x.xx5` rounds **up**, minting a sub-cent. Conversely a row can reach `remaining_amount = 0.00` while `status` stays `'active'` (because `newRemaining` was a tiny non-zero residue) — harmless only because every read also carries `remaining_amount > 0` (`:318`, `:505`, `:565`, `:687`). Structurally, four separate query sites must each remember that guard for the `status` field to be non-load-bearing.\n\n**consequence** — Sub-cent credit creation per grant-touching operation, plus a `status`/`remaining_amount` pair that can disagree. Money impact is tiny; the concern is that the ledger's 2dp invariant is enforced only as a side effect of the column type, not by the code.\n\n**confidence** low-medium\n\n---\n\n## depth_limits\n\n- **Not verified against a live Postgres.** The Read-Committed `FOR UPDATE` EvalPlanQual re-check at `db-layer.ts:560-581` looks correct to me on paper (the re-read after lock acquisition should return post-update `remaining_amount`, and re-apply the `remaining_amount > 0` qualifier), so I did **not** file a same-key double-spend bug at the DB layer — the route's `:117` comment appears accurate.
